\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{31}^{-1}\mathfrak{R}_{21}^{-1}[\mathfrak{p}_{13}\eqref{YB-noparam}]\mathfrak{R}_{21}^{-1}\mathfrak{R}_{31}^{-1}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{31}^{-1}[\mathfrak{p}_{23}

\displaystyle\circ\mathfrak{p}_{12}\eqref{YB-noparam}]\mathfrak{R}_{31}^{-1}\ ,\qquad

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{23}^{-1}\mathfrak{R}_{21}^{-1}[\mathfrak{p}_{12}\eqref{YB-noparam}]\mathfrak{R}_{21}^{-1}\mathfrak{R}_{23}^{-1}\ ,

and using (
4.74
), (
4.7
) with (
4.8
) and (
4.20
), while the unitarity property (
4.55
) is also used for the last one.
The coproduct, antipode and counit of
\mathcal{L}U_{q}sl_{2}
are given, respectively, by
14
14

14

The index
[j]
characterizes the ‘quantum space’
V_{[j]}
on which the entries of
{\bf L}^{\pm}(u)
act. With respect to the ordering
V_{[1]}\otimes V_{[2]}
, one has:

(4.77)

((T)_{[\mathsf{1}]}(T^{\prime})_{[\mathsf{2}]})_{ij}=\sum_{k=1}^{2}(T)_{ik}\otimes(T^{\prime})_{kj}\ .

:

(4.78)

\displaystyle(\Delta\otimes{\text{id}})({\bf L}^{\pm}(u))

\displaystyle=\left({\bf L}^{\pm}(u)\right)_{[1]}\left({\bf L}^{\pm}(u)\right)_{[2]}\ ,

(4.79)

\displaystyle(S\otimes{\text{id}})({\bf L}^{\pm}(u))

\displaystyle=({\bf L}^{\pm}(u))^{-1}\ ,

(4.80)

\displaystyle(\epsilon\otimes{\text{id}})({\bf L}^{\pm}(u))

\displaystyle=1\ .

These relations are easily understood using (
R2
) and (
2.1
)-(
2.2
): the relation (
4.78
) is obtained by applying
({\text{id}}\otimes{\text{id}}\otimes\pi_{u^{-1}}^{\frac{1}{2}})
on (
R2
), and similarly for
{\bf L}^{-}(u)
using
(\pi_{u^{-1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\otimes{\text{id}}\otimes{\text{id}})\circ({\text{id}}\otimes\Delta)(\mathfrak{R}^{-1})
.
The relations (
4.79
), (
4.80
) follow immediately from (
2.1
), (
2.2
).

Consider the subalgebra in
B
generated by the matrix entries of the K-operator
{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
. They satisfy the reflection equation (
4.67
) for
j_{1}=j_{2}=\frac{1}{2}
. Similarly to the coproduct of
\mathcal{L}U_{q}sl_{2}
discussed above, the coaction for this subalgebra can be expressed in terms of L- and K-operators.

Proposition 4.20
.

The coaction map
\delta\colon B\rightarrow B\otimes\mathcal{L}U_{q}sl_{2}
restricted to the subalgebra generated by the matrix entries of
{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
is such that

(4.81)

(\delta\otimes{\text{id}})({\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u))=\left([{\bf L}^{-}(u^{-1})]^{-1}\right)_{[2]}\left({\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\right)_{[1]}\left({\bf L}^{+}(u)\right)_{[2]}.

Proof.

From the fundamental axiom (
K2
), the l.h.s. of (
4.81
) can be written as

({\text{id}}\otimes{\text{id}}\otimes\pi_{u^{-1}}^{\frac{1}{2}})\circ(\delta\otimes{\text{id}})(\mathfrak{K})=({\text{id}}\otimes{\text{id}}\otimes\pi_{u^{-1}}^{\frac{1}{2}})\circ((\mathfrak{R}^{\eta})_{32}\mathfrak{K}_{13}\mathfrak{R}_{23})\ ,

where
(\mathfrak{R}^{\eta})_{32}=({\text{id}}\otimes{\text{id}}\otimes\eta)(\mathfrak{R}_{32})
. Then, using
\pi_{u^{-1}}^{j}\circ\eta=\pi_{u}^{j}
together with the definition of the K-operator and
{\bf L}^{\pm}(u)
given respectively in (
4.56
), (
4.74
), the claim follows.
∎

In the case when
B
is generated by the matrix entries of the K-operator
{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
, eq. (
4.81
) expresses the coaction map for
B
solely in terms of L- and K-operators. This is the case when
B=\mathcal{A}_{q}
and it will be discussed in Section
6
.

Let us now introduce a formal generalization of the evaluated coaction (
2.46
)

(4.82)

\delta_{w}=({\text{id}}\otimes{\mathsf{ev}}_{w})\circ\delta\colon\quad B\rightarrow B\otimes U_{q}sl_{2}[w^{\pm 1}]

with a formal variable
w
. We will nevertheless call
\delta_{w}

the evaluated coaction
for brevity.
We now consider the evaluated coaction applied to the matrix elements of the spin-
j
K-operator, which is obtained by taking the image of (
K2
) under the formal evaluation map
({\text{id}}\otimes\textsf{ev}_{w}\otimes\pi_{u^{-1}}^{j})
using (
4.1
), (
4.23
), (
4.57
), and is given by

(4.83)

(\delta_{w}\otimes{\text{id}})({\bf K}^{(j)}(u))=\left({\bf L}^{(j)}(u/w)\right)_{[2]}\left({\bf K}^{(j)}(u)\right)_{[1]}\left({\bf L}^{(j)}(uw)\right)_{[2]}\ .

Whereas the action of
({\text{id}}\otimes\pi^{j}_{v^{-1}})
on (
K1
) gives

(4.84)

{\bf K}^{(j)}(v)({\text{id}}\otimes\pi^{j})[\delta_{v^{-1}}(b)]=({\text{id}}\otimes\pi^{j})[\delta_{v}(b)]{\bf K}^{(j)}(v)\ .

We call this equation
the twisted intertwining relation
for
{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)
.

5.
Fused K-operators for
{\cal A}_{q}

In this section, we consider the comodule algebra
B=\mathcal{A}_{q}
and related ‘fused’ K-operators. Contrary to the previous section, here we do not assume the existence of a universal K-matrix. Instead, we introduce the fundamental K-operator for
B=\mathcal{A}_{q}
and recall the corresponding Faddeev-Reshetikhin-Taktadjan type presentation following
[
BS09
,
BB17
]
. Then, in Section
5.2
, fused K-operators
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)
built from the fundamental K-operator by analogy with (
4.72
) are shown to satisfy the reflection equation (
4.67
) for all
j\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{N}_{+}
where
{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)
is replaced by
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)
. This is the main result in this section. We also establish the unitarity and invertibility properties of
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)
in Section
5.3
, and examples of the fused K-operators are derived explicitly for small values of
j
in Section
5.4
. In preparation to the discussion in the next section, in Section
5.5
we calculate the evaluated coaction for
\mathcal{A}_{q}
and also establish in Section
5.6
the twisted intertwining relations for the fused K-operators which are similar to (
4.84
).

5.1.
The fundamental K-operator for
\mathcal{A}_{q}

An alternative presentation for
\mathcal{A}_{q}
besides Definition
2.14
is known, which takes the form
of a reflection algebra
[
BS09
]
that is recalled below.
Note that part of the material in this subsection is taken from
[
BS09
,
BB17
,
T21a
]
.
Let
R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u)
be the symmetric
R
-matrix (
4.8
)
which satisfies the quantum Yang-Baxter equation (
4.11
) with the substitution
\mathcal{R}^{(j_{k},j_{\ell})}(u)\rightarrow R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u)
.
We now introduce the K-operator that provides the
reflection algebra
presentation of
{\cal A}_{q}
, with the parametrization from (
2.43
).

Theorem 5.1

(
[
BS09
]
)
.

\cal A_{q}
admits a presentation in the form of a reflection algebra. Introduce the generating functions:

(5.1)

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{+}(u)=\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}{{\mathsf{W}}}_{-k}U^{-k-1}\ ,\quad{\cal W}_{-}(u)=\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}{{\mathsf{W}}}_{k+1}U^{-k-1}\ ,

(5.2)

\displaystyle\quad{\cal G}_{+}(u)=\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}{{\mathsf{G}}}_{k+1}U^{-k-1}\ ,\;\quad{\cal G}_{-}(u)=\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\tilde{{{\mathsf{G}}}}_{k+1}}U^{-k-1}\ ,\;

where the shorthand notation
U=(qu^{2}+q^{-1}u^{-2})/(q+q^{-1})
is used. The defining relations are given by:

(5.3)

\displaystyle R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u/v)\ {\cal K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\ R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(uv)\ {\cal K}_{2}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(v)\ =\ {\cal K}_{2}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(v)\ R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(uv)\ {\cal K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\ R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u/v)\

with the R-matrix from (
4.8
) and

(5.4)

{\cal K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)=\begin{pmatrix}uq\cal W_{+}(u)-u^{-1}q^{-1}\cal W_{-}(u)&\frac{1}{k_{-}(q+q^{-1})}\cal G_{+}(u)+\frac{k_{+}(q+q^{-1})}{q-q^{-1}}\\
\frac{1}{k_{+}(q+q^{-1})}\cal G_{-}(u)+\frac{k_{-}(q+q^{-1})}{q-q^{-1}}&uq\cal W_{-}(u)-u^{-1}q^{-1}\cal W_{+}(u)\end{pmatrix}\ ,

and with the identification (
2.43
).

We call
\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)

the fundamental K-operator
for
\mathcal{A}_{q}
.
We note that
U^{-1}
can be written as a power series in
u^{-2}
with the following expansion

(5.5)

U^{-1}=(1+q^{-2})u^{-2}\displaystyle{\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}(-u^{-4}q^{-2})^{\ell}}\ .

Thus, the generating functions
\mathcal{W}_{\pm}(u)
,
\mathcal{G}_{\pm}(u)
start with
u^{-2}
. Consequently, the leading term of the diagonal entries of the K-operator in (
5.4
) is at
u^{-1}
, while the leading term of the off-diagonal entries is at
u^{0}
. Therefore,
\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
is in
\mathcal{A}_{q}[[u^{-1}]]\otimes\text{\rm End}(\mathbb{C}^{2})
.

When we write
{\cal W}_{\pm}(u^{-1})
and
{\cal G}_{\pm}(u^{-1})
it means replacing
u
by
u^{-1}
in
U
and developing in power series in
u^{-2}
, this way we get

(5.6)

{\cal W}_{\pm}(u^{-1})={\cal W}_{\pm}(uq^{-1})\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\cal G}_{\pm}(u^{-1})={\cal G}_{\pm}(uq^{-1})\ .

In particular, this shows that
\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u^{-1})
is equally in
\mathcal{A}_{q}[[u^{-1}]]\otimes\text{\rm End}(\mathbb{C}^{2})
.

Explicitly, in terms of the generating functions (
5.1
), (
5.2
) the defining relations (
5.3
) read:

(5.7)

\displaystyle\left[\cal W_{\pm}(u),\cal W_{\pm}(v)\right]

\displaystyle=0\ ,

(5.8)

\displaystyle\left[\cal W_{+}(u),\cal W_{-}(v)\right]

\displaystyle+\left[\cal W_{-}(u),\cal W_{+}(v)\right]=0\ ,

(5.9)

\displaystyle\left[\cal G_{\epsilon}(u),\cal W_{\pm}(v)\right]+\left[\cal W_{\pm}(u),\cal G_{\epsilon}(v)\right]=0,\quad\epsilon=\pm\ ,

(5.10)

\displaystyle\left[\cal G_{\pm}(u),\cal G_{\pm}(v)\right]=0\ ,

(5.11)

\displaystyle\left[\cal G_{+}(u),\cal G_{-}(v)\right]+\left[\cal G_{-}(u),\cal G_{+}(v)\right]=0\ ,

(5.12)

\displaystyle(U-V)\left[\cal W_{\pm}(u),\cal W_{\mp}(v)\right]=\frac{(q-q^{-1})}{\rho(q+q^{-1})}\left(\cal G_{\pm}(u)\cal G_{\mp}(v)-\cal G_{\pm}(v)\cal G_{\mp}(u)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\frac{1}{(q+q^{-1})}\left(\cal G_{\pm}(u)-\cal G_{\mp}(u)+\cal G_{\mp}(v)-\cal G_{\pm}(v)\right)\ ,

(5.13)

\displaystyle\cal W_{\pm}(u)\cal W_{\pm}(v)-\cal W_{\mp}(u)\cal W_{\mp}(v)+\frac{1}{\rho(q^{2}-q^{-2})}\left[\cal G_{\pm}(u),\cal G_{\mp}(v)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\frac{1-UV}{U-V}\left(\cal W_{\pm}(u)\cal W_{\mp}(v)-\cal W_{\pm}(v)\cal W_{\mp}(u)\right)=0\ ,

(5.14)

\displaystyle U\left[\cal G_{\mp}(v),\cal W_{\pm}(u)\right]_{q}-V\left[\cal G_{\mp}(u),\cal W_{\pm}(v)\right]_{q}-(q-q^{-1})\left(\cal W_{\mp}(u)\cal G_{\mp}(v)-\cal W_{\mp}(v)\cal G_{\mp}(u)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\rho\left(U\cal W_{\pm}(u)-V\cal W_{\pm}(v)-\cal W_{\mp}(u)+\cal W_{\mp}(v)\right)=0\ ,

(5.15)

\displaystyle U\left[\cal W_{\mp}(u),\cal G_{\mp}(v)\right]_{q}-V\left[\cal W_{\mp}(v),\cal G_{\mp}(u)\right]_{q}-(q-q^{-1})\left(\cal W_{\pm}(u)\cal G_{\mp}(v)-\cal W_{\pm}(v)\cal G_{\mp}(u)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\rho\left(U\cal W_{\mp}(u)-V\cal W_{\mp}(v)-\cal W_{\pm}(u)+\cal W_{\pm}(v)\right)=0\ .

We note that the corresponding relations for the modes
{{\mathsf{W}}}_{0}
,
{{\mathsf{W}}}_{1}
,
\{{{\mathsf{G}}}_{k+1}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
give the compact presentation from Definition
2.14
. The modes
\{{{\mathsf{W}}}_{-k}
,
{{\mathsf{W}}}_{k+1}
,
{{\mathsf{G}}}_{k+1}
,
\tilde{{{\mathsf{G}}}}_{k+1}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
give actually
PBW type generators, called
alternating
.
The expressions of the alternating generators
{\mathsf{W}}_{-k}
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{k+1}
in terms of the generators of the compact presentation (called
essential
generators in
[
T21b
]
) are obtained recursively from two of the defining relations of
{\cal A}_{q}

[
BS09
, Def. 3.1 & eqs. (3.2),(3.3)]
:

(5.16)

\displaystyle{{\mathsf{W}}}_{-k-1}=

\displaystyle{{\mathsf{W}}}_{k+1}+\frac{1}{\rho}\big[{{\mathsf{W}}}_{0},{{\mathsf{G}}}_{k+1}\big]_{q}\ ,

(5.17)

\displaystyle{{\mathsf{W}}}_{k+2}=

\displaystyle{{\mathsf{W}}}_{-k}+\frac{1}{\rho}\big[{{{\mathsf{G}}}}_{k+1},{{\mathsf{W}}}_{1}\big]_{q}\ .

We introduce the following automorphism of
\mathcal{A}_{q}
:

(5.18)

\sigma\colon\qquad\cal W_{\pm}(u)\to\cal W_{\mp}(u)\ ,\quad\cal G_{\pm}(u)\to\cal G_{\mp}(u)\ ,\quad k_{\pm}\to k_{\mp}\ .

It is obtained from the reflection equation (
5.3
). Indeed, note that the R-matrix is such that
{\cal R}^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u)=M{\cal R}^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u)M
, with
M=\sigma_{x}\otimes\sigma_{x}
. Consider the conjugation of the K-operator by
\sigma_{x}
. Its entries read:
(\sigma_{x}{\cal K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\sigma_{x})_{i,j}=({\cal K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u))_{3-i,3-j}
for
1\leq i,j\leq 2
. Then, multiplying (
5.3
) on both sides by
M\otimes M
, the automorphism
\sigma
follows.

In the following, we need the so-called quantum determinant associated with the K-operator. It is a generating function for central elements of
\mathcal{A}_{q}
, given by
[
Sk88
]
:

(5.19)

\Gamma(u)={\text{tr}}_{12}\big({\cal P}^{-}_{12}{\cal K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(qu^{2}){\cal K}_{2}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq)\big)\ ,

where
\mathcal{P}^{-}
is defined below (
4.12
).

Proposition 5.2

(
[
BB17
,
T21a
]
)
.

The quantum determinant of the fundamental K-operator

(5.20)

\Gamma(u)=\frac{(u^{2}q^{2}-u^{-2}q^{-2})}{2(q-q^{-1})}\left(\Delta^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)-\frac{2\rho}{q-q^{-1}}\right)\ ,

with

\displaystyle\Delta^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)

\displaystyle=-(q-q^{-1})(q^{2}+q^{-2})\Big(\cal W_{+}(u)\cal W_{+}(uq)+\cal W_{-}(u)\cal W_{-}(uq)\Big)

\displaystyle+(q-q^{-1})(u^{2}q^{2}+u^{-2}q^{-2})\Big(\cal W_{+}(u)\cal W_{-}(uq)+\cal W_{-}(u)\cal W_{+}(uq)\Big)

(5.21)

\displaystyle-\frac{(q-q^{-1})}{\rho}\Big(\cal G_{+}(u)\cal G_{-}(uq)+\cal G_{-}(u)\cal G_{+}(uq)\Big)-\ \cal G_{+}(u)-\cal G_{+}(uq)-\cal G_{-}(u)-\cal G_{-}(uq)\ ,

is such that
\big[\Gamma(u),{\cal K}_{mn}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(v)\big]=0
. It generates the center of
{\cal A}_{q}
, denoted by
Z({\cal A}_{q})
.

We can expand
\Delta^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
as a formal power series in
u^{-2}

(5.22)

\Delta^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)=\displaystyle{\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}}u^{-2k-2}c_{k+1}\Delta_{k+1}\ ,

where

(5.23)

c_{k}=-q^{-2k}(q+q^{-1})^{k}(q^{k}+q^{-k})\ .

An explicit formula expressing the central elements
\Delta_{k+1}
in terms of the alternating generators we introduced above is given in
[
BB17
, Lem. 2.1]
. Here, we give a few of them, as they are used below.

Example 5.3
.

First few modes from (
5.22
), as elements of
Z({\cal A}_{q})
, are

(5.24)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\Delta_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathsf{G}}_{1}+{\tilde{{{\mathsf{G}}}}_{1}}-(q-q^{-1})\big({\mathsf{W}}_{0}{\mathsf{W}}_{1}+{\mathsf{W}}_{1}{\mathsf{W}}_{0}\big),

(5.25)

\displaystyle\Delta_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathsf{G}}_{2}+{\tilde{{{\mathsf{G}}}}_{2}}-\frac{(q^{2}-q^{-2})}{(q^{2}+q^{-2})}(q^{-1}{\mathsf{W}}_{0}{\mathsf{W}}_{2}+q{\mathsf{W}}_{2}{\mathsf{W}}_{0}+q^{-1}{\mathsf{W}}_{1}{\mathsf{W}}_{-1}+q{\mathsf{W}}_{-1}{\mathsf{W}}_{1})\,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{(q-q^{-1})}{(q^{2}+q^{-2})}\,\Big((q^{2}+q^{-2})({\mathsf{W}}_{0}^{2}+{\mathsf{W}}_{1}^{2})+\frac{\tilde{{\mathsf{G}}}_{1}{\mathsf{G}}_{1}+{\mathsf{G}}_{1}\tilde{{\mathsf{G}}}_{1}}{\rho}\Big)\ ,

where
{\mathsf{W}}_{-1}
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{2}
are determined by (
5.16
) and (
5.17
) at
k=0
, respectively.

Lemma 5.4
.

(5.26)

\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u^{-1})\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)=\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u^{-1})=\frac{\Gamma(uq^{-1})}{c(u^{-2})}\,\mathbb{I}_{2}\ ,

where
c(u)
and
\Gamma(u)
are respectively given in (
3.42
) and (
5.20
), and where the right hand side we consider as an element in
Z(\mathcal{A}_{q})[[u^{-1}]]
.

Proof.

Using our developing in series convention in (
5.6
) and the ordering relations of
\mathcal{A}_{q}
given in Appendix
C
, it is straightforward to calculate (
5.26
).
∎

We will call the above property (
5.26
) the unitarity property of the fundamental K-operator by analogy with the L-operator.

Let us now show invertibility property of the fundamental K-operator
\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
from (
5.4
).
We first show that
\Gamma(u)
is invertible in the ring
\mathcal{A}_{q}((u^{-1}))
. We notice that the constant term of
\Delta^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
from (
5.21
) is zero, which implies using (
5.20
) that the constant term of
u^{-2}\Gamma(u)
is non-zero. Therefore, the latter formal power series is invertible in
Z(\mathcal{A}_{q})[[u^{-1}]]
by
[
T21d
, Lem. 4.1]
. We thus obtain that
\Gamma(u)
is invertible in the ring
\mathcal{A}_{q}((u^{-1}))
. Explicit expressions of the modes of
\Gamma(u)^{-1}
can be deduced from
[
T21d
, Lem. 4.1]
shifting the mode index by
-2
.
Since
\Gamma(u)
is invertible, it follows by Lemma
5.4
that
\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
is invertible too and its inverse is given by:

(5.27)

\left[\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\right]^{-1}=\frac{c(u^{-2})}{\Gamma(uq^{-1})}\,\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u^{-1})\ .

Remark 5.5
.

A central element of
\mathcal{A}_{q}
denoted
\mathcal{Z}(t)
has been proposed
[
T21a
, Def. 8.4]
. It is easily checked that adapting its expression to our conventions, one has

(5.28)

\frac{\Gamma(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})}{c(u^{2}q)}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}(t)\ ,

with the identification

\displaystyle\rho\rightarrow-(q^{2}-q^{-2})^{2}\ ,\qquad q^{-1}u^{-2}\rightarrow t\ ,\qquad\mathcal{W}_{\mp}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\rightarrow S\mathcal{W}^{\pm}(S)\ ,\qquad\mathcal{W}_{\mp}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})\rightarrow T\mathcal{W}^{\pm}(T)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{+}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})+\rho/(q-q^{-1})\rightarrow\mathcal{G}(S)\ ,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{G}_{-}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})+\rho/(q-q^{-1})\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{G}}(T)\ .

5.2.
Fused K-operators for
\mathcal{A}_{q}

Recall
R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u)
and the fundamental K-operator
{\mathcal{K}}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
, given respectively in (
4.8
) and (
5.4
), satisfy the reflection equation (
5.3
). By analogy with (
4.72
), we now introduce fused K-operators
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)\in\mathcal{A}_{q}((u^{-1}))\otimes\text{\rm End}(\mathbb{C}^{2j+1})
:

Definition 5.6
.

For
j\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{N}_{+}
, the fused K-operators for
\mathcal{A}_{q}
are

(5.29)

{\cal K}^{(j+\frac{1}{2})}(u)=\mathcal{F}^{(j+\frac{1}{2})}_{\langle 12\rangle}{\cal K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{-j})R^{(\frac{1}{2},j)}(u^{2}q^{-j+\frac{1}{2}}){\cal K}_{2}^{(j)}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{E}^{(j+\frac{1}{2})}_{\langle 12\rangle},

with
{\cal K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
defined in (
5.4
). We note that
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)\in u^{4j^{2}-2j}\mathcal{A}_{q}[[u^{-1}]]\otimes\text{\rm End}(\mathbb{C}^{2j+1})
.

The following Theorem is our second main result.

Theorem 5.7
.

The fused K-operators
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)
satisfy the reflection equation for any
j_{1}
,
j_{2}\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{N}_{+}
:

(5.30)

\displaystyle R^{(j_{1},j_{2})}(u_{1}/u_{2})

\displaystyle{\cal K}^{(j_{1})}_{1}(u_{1})R^{(j_{1},j_{2})}(u_{1}u_{2}){\cal K}_{2}^{(j_{2})}(u_{2})=

\displaystyle{\cal K}_{2}^{(j_{2})}(u_{2})R^{(j_{1},j_{2})}(u_{1}u_{2}){\cal K}_{1}^{(j_{1})}(u_{1})R^{(j_{1},j_{2})}(u_{1}/u_{2})\ .

The proof of this theorem is very technical and was delegated to Appendix
D
. We proceed by induction on
j_{1}
,
j_{2}
, using
several expressions for the fused R-matrices
R^{(j_{1},j_{2})}(u)
defined in (
4.37
).
Moreover, the decomposition of
R^{(\frac{1}{2},j)}(q^{j+\frac{1}{2}})
, expressed in terms of
\mathcal{E}^{(j+\frac{1}{2})}
,
\mathcal{F}^{(j+\frac{1}{2})}
, and
\mathcal{H}^{(j+1/2)}
as given in Lemma
3.5
, is crucial for the proof of this theorem, along with the relations from Corollary
3.6
.

5.3.
Unitarity and invertibility properties

We now discuss the unitarity and invertibility properties of the fused K-operators
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)
defined by (
5.29
). Recall that
\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
satisfies the unitarity property and is invertible, see Lemma
5.4
and expression in (
5.27
), respectively. We generalize these properties for any spin-
j
.

Proposition 5.8
.

Let

(5.31)

\widehat{\mathcal{K}}^{(j+\frac{1}{2})}(u)=\mathcal{F}^{(j+\frac{1}{2})}_{\langle 12\rangle}\widehat{\mathcal{K}}^{(j)}_{2}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})R^{(\frac{1}{2},j)}(u^{2}q^{j-\frac{1}{2}})\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{j})\mathcal{E}^{(j+\frac{1}{2})}_{\langle 12\rangle}\ ,

for
j\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{N}_{+}
and with
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\equiv\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
. Then

(5.32)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)\widehat{\mathcal{K}}^{(j)}(u^{-1})

\displaystyle=\left(\displaystyle{\prod_{k=0}^{2j-1}\frac{\Gamma(uq^{-j-\frac{1}{2}+k})}{c(u^{-2}q^{2j-1-2k})}}\right)\!\left(\displaystyle{\prod_{k=0}^{2j-2}}\,\displaystyle{\prod_{\ell=0}^{2j-k-2}}c(u^{2}q^{2j-1-2k-\ell})c(u^{-2}q^{1-k+\ell})\right)\!\mathbb{I}_{2j+1}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathcal{K}}^{(j)}(u^{-1})\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)\ ,

where
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)
,
\Gamma(u)
and
c(u)
are respectively given in (
5.29
), (
5.20
), (
3.42
).

Proof.

Recall that (
5.32
) for
j=\frac{1}{2}
was proven in Lemma
5.4
. First, we show that (
5.32
) holds for
j=1
. Using (
5.29
) and (
5.31
), we find that the product
\mathcal{K}^{(1)}(u)\widehat{\mathcal{K}}^{(1)}(u^{-1})
equals

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{(1)}_{\langle 12\rangle}\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{1}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u^{2})\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{2}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{\langle 12\rangle}\mathcal{F}^{(1)}_{\langle 12\rangle}\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{2}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u^{-1}q^{-\frac{1}{2}})R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u^{-2})\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u^{-1}q^{\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{\langle 12\rangle}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{(1)}_{\langle 12\rangle}\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{1}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u^{2})\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{2}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{2}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u^{-1}q^{-\frac{1}{2}})R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u^{-2})\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u^{-1}q^{\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{\langle 12\rangle}\ ,

where we removed the product
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}\mathcal{F}^{(1)}
on the second line, similarly to the derivation of (
4.53
). Then, using (
3.43
) and (
5.26
) we have

\mathcal{K}^{(1)}(u)\widehat{\mathcal{K}}^{(1)}(u^{-1})=-\Gamma(uq^{-\frac{3}{2}})\Gamma(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})\,\mathbb{I}_{3}\ .

Similarly, we get

\widehat{\mathcal{K}}^{(1)}(u^{-1})\mathcal{K}^{(1)}(u)=-\Gamma(uq^{-\frac{3}{2}})\Gamma(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})\,\mathbb{I}_{3}\ .

More generally, by induction we get both equalities in (
5.32
) for all spin
j
.
∎

Remark 5.9
.

The spin-
j
fused K-operator
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)
is invertible and its inverse lies in
\mathcal{A}_{q}[[u^{-1}]]\otimes\text{\rm End}(\mathbb{C}^{2j+1})
and is given by:

(5.33)

\left[\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)\right]^{-1}\!\!=\left[\displaystyle{\prod_{k=0}^{2j-1}\!\frac{\Gamma(uq^{-j-\frac{1}{2}+k})}{c(u^{-2}q^{2j-1-2k})}}\right]^{-1}\!\left[\displaystyle{\prod_{k=0}^{2j-2}}\,\displaystyle{\prod_{\ell=0}^{2j-k-2}}\!\!c(u^{2}q^{2j-1-2k-\ell})c(u^{-2}q^{1-k+\ell})\right]^{-1}\!\!\!\!\widehat{\mathcal{K}}^{(j)}(u^{-1}).

Remark 5.10
.

By direct calculations we have checked for
j=1,\frac{3}{2},2
that
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}^{(j)}(u)
is equal to
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)
defined in (
5.29
) and we expect this equality holds for any
j
. Note that
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)
and
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}^{(j)}(u)
, are direct analogs of the spin-
j
K-operators
{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)
defined in (
4.72
) and (
4.68
), respectively.

5.4.
Examples of fused K-operators

In this subsection we give examples of spin-
1
and spin-
\frac{3}{2}
fused K-operators
{\cal K}^{(j)}(u)
for
{\cal A}_{q}
, defined by (
5.29
). Recall the function
c(u)
given in (
3.42
).

5.4.1.
Spin-
1
fused K-operator

The expressions of
\mathcal{E}^{(j+\frac{1}{2})}
,
\mathcal{F}^{(j+\frac{1}{2})}
in (
3.16
), (
3.17
), for
j=\frac{1}{2}
read:

\mathcal{E}^{(1)}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&\frac{1}{\sqrt{[2]_{q}}}&0\\
0&\frac{1}{\sqrt{[2]_{q}}}&0\\
0&0&1\end{pmatrix},\qquad\mathcal{F}^{(1)}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0&0\\
0&\frac{\sqrt{[2]_{q}}}{2}&\frac{\sqrt{[2]_{q}}}{2}&0\\
0&0&0&1\end{pmatrix}.

From (
4.38
), the fused R-matrix reads

(5.34)

\displaystyle R^{\left(\frac{1}{2},1\right)}(u)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{(1)}_{\langle 23\rangle}R_{13}^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})R_{12}^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{\langle 23\rangle}

and is given explicitly by

(5.35)

R^{\left(\frac{1}{2},1\right)}(u)=c(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\begin{pmatrix}c(uq^{\frac{3}{2}})&0&0&0&0&0\\
0&c(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})&0&c(q)\sqrt{[2]_{q}}&0&0\\
0&0&c(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})&0&c(q)\sqrt{[2]_{q}}&0\\
0&c(q)\sqrt{[2]_{q}}&0&c(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})&0&0\\
0&0&c(q)\sqrt{[2]_{q}}&0&c(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})&0\\
0&0&0&0&0&c(uq^{\frac{3}{2}})\end{pmatrix}\ .

From (
5.29
), the fused K-operator is given by:

(5.36)

{\cal K}^{(1)}(u)=\mathcal{F}^{(1)}_{\langle 12\rangle}{\cal K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u^{2}){\cal K}_{2}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{\langle 12\rangle}\ .

Using the above expressions, one finds that the entries
{\cal K}_{mn}^{(1)}(u)
are explicitly given by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{(1)}_{11}(u)

\displaystyle=\big(c(q)^{-1}+\rho^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{+}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})\big)\big(\rho+c(q)\mathcal{G}_{-}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\big)

\displaystyle+c(u^{2}q)\big(uq^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{W}_{+}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})-u^{-1}q^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{W}_{-}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})\big)\big(uq^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{W}_{+}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})-u^{-1}q^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{W}_{-}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{(1)}_{12}(u)

\displaystyle=\frac{(q+q^{-1})^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{k_{-}}\Big(c(u^{2})\big(\rho c(q)^{-1}+\mathcal{G}_{+}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})\big)\big(uq^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{W}_{+}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})-u^{-1}q^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{W}_{-}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\big)

\displaystyle+\big(\rho+c(q)\mathcal{G}_{+}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})\big)\big(uq^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{W}_{-}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})-u^{-1}q^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{W}_{-}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\big)

\displaystyle+c(u^{2}q)\big(uq^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{W}_{+}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})-u^{-1}q^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{W}_{-}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})\big)\big(\rho c(q)^{-1}+\mathcal{G}_{+}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\big)\Big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{(1)}_{13}(u)

\displaystyle=\frac{c(u^{2})}{k_{-}^{2}c(q^{2})^{2}}\big(\rho+c(q)\mathcal{G}_{+}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})\big)\big(\rho+c(q)\mathcal{G}_{+}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\big),

(5.37)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{(1)}_{21}(u)

\displaystyle=\frac{c(q)^{-1}}{2k_{+}\sqrt{q+q^{-1}}}\Big(c(u^{2}q)\big(\rho+c(q)\mathcal{G}_{-}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})\big)\big(uq^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{W}_{+}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})-u^{-1}q^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{W}_{-}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\big)

\displaystyle+\big(q^{-\frac{1}{2}}(u^{-3}+u(-2+q^{2}))\mathcal{W}_{-}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})+q^{\frac{1}{2}}(u^{3}+u^{-1}(-2+q^{-2}))\mathcal{W}_{+}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\times\big(\rho+c(q)\mathcal{G}_{-}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{(1)}_{22}(u)

\displaystyle=\frac{c(u^{2}q)}{2c(q)^{2}\rho}\Big(\big(\rho+c(q)\mathcal{G}_{+}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})\big)\big(\rho+c(q)\mathcal{G}_{-}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\big(\rho+c(q)\mathcal{G}_{-}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})\big)\big(\rho+c(q)\mathcal{G}_{+}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\big(q^{-\frac{1}{2}}(u^{-3}+u(-2+q^{2}))\mathcal{W}_{+}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})+q^{\frac{1}{2}}(u^{-1}(-2+q^{2})+u^{3})\mathcal{W}_{-}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})\big)

\displaystyle\times\big(uq^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{W}_{+}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})-u^{-1}q^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{W}_{-}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\big(q^{-\frac{1}{2}}(u^{-3}+u(-2+q^{2}))\mathcal{W}_{-}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})+q^{\frac{1}{2}}(u^{-1}(-2+q^{2})+u^{3})\mathcal{W}_{+}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})\big)

\displaystyle\times\big(uq^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{W}_{-}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})-u^{-1}q^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{W}_{+}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\big),

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt{\cal K}^{(1)}_{23}(u)=\sigma({\cal K}^{(1)}_{21}(u))\ ,\qquad{\cal K}^{(1)}_{31}(u)=\sigma({\cal K}^{(1)}_{13}(u))\ ,

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt{\cal K}^{(1)}_{32}(u)=\sigma({\cal K}^{(1)}_{12}(u))\ ,\qquad{\cal K}^{(1)}_{33}(u)=\sigma({\cal K}^{(1)}_{11}(u))\ ,

where
\sigma
is defined in (
5.18
). The last two lines describe the exchange of the entries of the fused K-operator due to the automorphism
\sigma
and can be seen graphically:

(5.38)

\mathcal{K}^{(1)}(u)=
.
\mathcal{K}_{11}^{(1)}(u)
\mathcal{K}_{12}^{(1)}(u)
\mathcal{K}_{13}^{(1)}(u)
\mathcal{K}_{21}^{(1)}(u)
\mathcal{K}_{22}^{(1)}(u)
\mathcal{K}_{23}^{(1)}(u)
\mathcal{K}_{31}^{(1)}(u)
\mathcal{K}_{32}^{(1)}(u)
\mathcal{K}_{33}^{(1)}(u)
(
)

As shown in Lemma
D.3
, the fused K-operator (
6.1
) for
j=1
satisfies the reflection equation:

(5.39)

R^{(\frac{1}{2},1)}(u/v)\mathcal{K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)R^{(\frac{1}{2},1)}(uv)\mathcal{K}_{2}^{(1)}(v)=\mathcal{K}_{2}^{(1)}(v)R^{(\frac{1}{2},1)}(uv)\mathcal{K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)R^{(\frac{1}{2},1)}(u/v)\ .

Note that the latter equation can be independently checked using the ordering relations given in Lemma
C.1
.

5.4.2.
Spin-
\frac{3}{2}
fused K-operator

The elements
\mathcal{E}^{(j+\frac{1}{2})},\mathcal{F}^{(j+\frac{1}{2})}
in (
3.16
), (
3.17
) for
j=1
read:

\mathcal{E}^{(\frac{3}{2})}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0&0\\
0&\frac{\sqrt{[2]_{q}}}{\sqrt{[3]_{q}}}&0&0\\
0&0&\frac{[2]_{q}}{\sqrt{([2]_{q})^{2}}\sqrt{[3]_{q}}}&0\\
0&\frac{1}{\sqrt{[3]_{q}}}&0&0\\
0&0&\frac{([2]_{q})^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\sqrt{([2]_{q})^{2}}\sqrt{[3]_{q}}}&0\\
0&0&0&\frac{[2]_{q}}{\sqrt{([2]_{q})^{2}}}\end{pmatrix},\mathcal{F}^{(\frac{3}{2})}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0&0&0&0\\
0&\frac{\sqrt{[2]_{q}}\sqrt{[3]_{q}}}{1+[2]_{q}}&0&\frac{\sqrt{[3]_{q}}}{1+[2]_{q}}&0&0\\
0&0&\frac{\sqrt{[3]_{q}}\sqrt{([2]_{q})^{2}}}{[2]_{q}(1+[2]_{q})}&0&\frac{\sqrt{[3]_{q}}\sqrt{([2]_{q})^{2}}}{\sqrt{[2]_{q}}(1+[2]_{q})}&0\\
0&0&0&0&0&\frac{\sqrt{([2]_{q})^{2}}}{[2]_{q}}\end{pmatrix}.

The fused R-matrix from (
4.38
) reads

\displaystyle R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2})}(u)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{(\frac{3}{2})}_{\langle 23\rangle}R_{13}^{\left(\frac{1}{2},1\right)}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})R_{12}^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(uq)\mathcal{E}^{(\frac{3}{2})}_{\langle 23\rangle}\ ,

given explicitly by

(5.40)

R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2})}(u)=c(u)c(uq)c(q)\begin{pmatrix}\frac{c(uq^{2})}{c(q)}&0&0&0&0&0&0&0\\
0&\frac{c(uq)}{c(q)}&0&0&\sqrt{[3]_{q}}&0&0&0\\
0&0&\frac{c(u)}{c(q)}&0&0&\sqrt{([2]_{q})^{2}}&0&0\\
0&0&0&\frac{c(uq^{-1})}{c(q)}&0&0&\sqrt{[3]_{q}}&0\\
0&\sqrt{[3]_{q}}&0&0&\frac{c(uq^{-1})}{c(q)}&0&0&0\\
0&0&\sqrt{([2]_{q})^{2}}&0&0&\frac{c(u)}{c(q)}&0&0\\
0&0&0&\sqrt{[3]_{q}}&0&0&\frac{c(uq)}{c(q)}&0\\
0&0&0&0&0&0&0&\frac{c(uq^{2})}{c(q)}\end{pmatrix}.

From (
5.29
), the fused K-operator reads:

(5.41)

{\cal K}^{(\frac{3}{2})}(u)=\mathcal{F}^{(\frac{3}{2})}_{\langle 12\rangle}{\cal K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{-1})R^{\left(\frac{1}{2},1\right)}(u^{2}q^{-\frac{1}{2}}){\cal K}_{2}^{(1)}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{E}^{(\frac{3}{2})}_{\langle 12\rangle}\ .

For instance, the first entry reads:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{3}{2})}_{11}(u)

\displaystyle=\frac{c(u^{2})(q+q^{-1})}{2\rho c(q)}\big(\rho+c(q)\mathcal{G}_{+}(uq^{-1})\big)\Big(c(u^{2}q^{2})\big(\rho+c(q)\mathcal{G}_{-}(u)\big)\big(uq^{2}\mathcal{W}_{+}(uq)-u^{-1}q^{-2}\mathcal{W}_{-}(uq)\big)

\displaystyle+u^{-1}\big((u^{2}(q^{2}-2)+u^{-2}q^{-2})\mathcal{W}_{-}(u)+(u^{4}q^{2}+q^{-2}-2)\mathcal{W}_{+}(u)\big)\big(\rho+c(q)\mathcal{G}_{-}(uq)\big)\Big)

\displaystyle+c(u^{2})c(u^{2}q)\big(u\mathcal{W}_{+}(uq^{-1})-u^{-1}\mathcal{W}_{-}(uq^{-1})\big)\Big(\big(\rho^{-1}\mathcal{G}_{+}(u)+c(q)^{-1}\big)\big(\rho+c(q)\mathcal{G}_{-}(uq)\big)

\displaystyle+c(u^{2}q^{2})\big(uq\mathcal{W}_{+}(u)-u^{-1}q^{-1}\mathcal{W}_{-}(u)\big)\big(uq^{2}\mathcal{W}_{+}(uq)-u^{-1}q^{-2}\mathcal{W}_{-}(uq)\big)\Big)\ .

The other explicit expressions of the entries in terms of the generating functions for
{\cal A}_{q}
are not reported here for simplicity. Under the action of
\sigma
from (
5.18
), the entries exchange according to:

(5.42)

By Theorem
5.7
, the fused K-operator satisfies the reflection equation:

R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2})}(u/v)\mathcal{K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2})}(uv)\mathcal{K}_{2}^{(\frac{3}{2})}(v)=\mathcal{K}_{2}^{(\frac{3}{2})}(v)R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2})}(uv)\mathcal{K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2})}(u/v)\ .

5.4.3.
Spin-
j
fused K-operator

Specializing the formula (
5.29
), one gets the fused K-operator
{\cal K}^{(j)}(u)
for any value of
j
starting from (
5.4
). By analogy with the previous cases, note that one has the invariance of the R-matrix (
3.41
)

R^{(\frac{1}{2},j)}(u)=M^{(j)}R^{(\frac{1}{2},j)}(u)M^{(j)}\qquad\mbox{with}\qquad M^{(j)}=\sigma_{x}\otimes\displaystyle{\sum_{n=1}^{2j+1}}E_{n,2j+2-n}^{(j,j)}\ .

So, due to the automorphism
\sigma
in (
5.18
), the entries of the K-operator of spin-
j
exchange as
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}_{m,n}(u)=\sigma(\mathcal{K}^{(j)}_{2j+2-m,2j+2-n}(u))
with
1\leq m,n\leq 2j+1
. This is analogous to the property in (
5.38
), (
5.42
).

From the fusion formulas (
4.38
) and (
5.29
) it is clear that the fused R-matrices and K-operators can be expressed only in terms of the fundamental K-operator and R-matrix, and the maps
\mathcal{E}^{(j)}
and
\mathcal{F}^{(j)}
. They are given by:

(5.43)

R^{(\frac{1}{2},j)}(u)\!=\!\left(\displaystyle{\prod_{m=0}^{2j-2}}\mathbb{I}_{2m+2}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{(j-\frac{m}{2})}\right)\!\!\left(\displaystyle{\prod_{k=0}^{2j-1}}R_{1\,2j+1-k}^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{-j+\frac{1}{2}+k})\right)\!\!\left(\displaystyle{\prod_{m=0}^{2j-2}}\mathbb{I}_{2(2j-1-m)}\otimes\mathcal{E}^{(1+\frac{m}{2})}\right),

and

(5.44)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)

\displaystyle=\!\left(\displaystyle{\prod_{m=0}^{2j-2}}\mathbb{I}_{2m}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{(j-\frac{m}{2})}\!\right)\!\displaystyle{\prod_{k=1}^{2j}}\!\left\{\!\mathcal{K}_{k}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{k-j-\frac{1}{2}})\!\left[\displaystyle{\prod_{\ell=0}^{2j-k-1}}R_{k\,2j-\ell}^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u^{2}q^{-2j+2k+\ell})\right]\!\right\}\!\!

\displaystyle\times\left(\displaystyle{\prod_{m=0}^{2j-2}}\mathbb{I}_{2(2j-2-m)}\otimes\mathcal{E}^{(1+\frac{m}{2})}\right)\ ,

where the product stands for the usual matrix product and the products are ordered from left to right in an increasing way in the indices.
The proof of (
5.43
) is straightforward by induction on
j
using (
4.38
), whereas the proof of (
5.44
) is more tedious.
We proceed by induction checking (
5.29
) using (
5.43
) and (
5.44
). Then, one obtains a formula similar to (
5.44
) for
j\rightarrow j+\frac{1}{2}
but with unwanted products
of
\mathcal{E}^{(j)}\mathcal{F}^{(j)}
. They can be removed using the same trick as in eq. (
4.53
).
Firstly, multiply (
5.29
) from the right by
\mathcal{H}^{(j+\frac{1}{2})}[\mathcal{H}^{(j+\frac{1}{2})}]^{-1}=\mathbb{I}_{2j+2}
and use (
3.48
) to move
\mathcal{H}^{(j+\frac{1}{2})}
to the left. Then, using successively the Yang-Baxter equation and the reflection equation, the products
\mathcal{E}^{(j)}\mathcal{F}^{(j)}
are removed using the property (
3.50
).

Note that in the literature, another fusion procedure was developed for the R-matrix, see
[
Ka79
,
KRS81
]
, and for the K-matrix in
[
MN92
]
. In this case, the analogue of the formulas (
5.44
), (
5.43
) can be found for instance in
[
FNR07
, eq. (2.1), (2.7)]
.

5.5.
Evaluated coaction of fused K-operators

The fused K-operators
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)
are expected to have a simple relation with the spin-
j
K-operators
{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)
as will be discussed in Section
6
, similarly to the relations between
{\bf L}^{(j)}(u)
and
\mathcal{L}^{(j)}(u)
. Therefore, the evaluated coaction
\delta_{w}
defined in (
4.82
) and applied to the entries of
\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
is expected to be of the form (
4.83
) up to appropriate normalization.

Lemma 5.11
.

The evaluated coaction

\delta_{w}\colon\mathcal{A}_{q}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{q}\otimes U_{q}sl_{2}[w^{\pm 1}]
is such that
15
15

15

Here, the index
[1]
is associated with the space for
{\cal A}_{q}
, and
[2]
for
U_{q}sl_{2}
, and we use the convention
((T)_{[\mathsf{2}]}(T^{\prime})_{[\mathsf{1}]}(T^{\prime\prime})_{[\mathsf{2}]})_{ij}=\sum_{k,\ell=1}^{2}(T^{\prime})_{k\ell}\otimes(T)_{ik}(T^{\prime\prime})_{\ell j}
.

(5.45)

(\delta_{w}\otimes{\text{id}})({\mathcal{K}}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u))=\frac{U^{-1}}{q+q^{-1}}\left({\cal L}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u/w)\right)_{[\mathsf{2}]}\left({\mathcal{K}}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\right)_{[\mathsf{1}]}\left({\cal L}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uw)\right)_{[\mathsf{2}]}\ .

Proof.

Assume the evaluated coaction takes the form

(5.46)

(\delta_{w}\otimes{\text{id}})({\mathcal{K}}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u))=f(u)\left({\cal L}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u/w)\right)_{[\mathsf{2}]}\left({\mathcal{K}}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\right)_{[\mathsf{1}]}\left({\cal L}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uw)\right)_{[\mathsf{2}]}

where
f(u)
is assumed to be invertible central in
{\cal A}_{q}[[u^{-1}]]\otimes U_{q}sl_{2}
. We show that
\delta_{w}
is indeed an algebra homomorphism for a certain choice of
f(u)
. It is easily checked using the Yang-Baxter algebra (
4.49
) satisfied by
\mathcal{L}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
that (
5.46
) solves the reflection equation (
5.3
) with the substitution
{\mathcal{K}}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\rightarrow(\delta_{w}\otimes{\text{id}})({\mathcal{K}}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u))
.
Then, we fix the function
f(u)
as follows. We compare the l.h.s. of (
5.46
) using (
5.4
) to the r.h.s. of (
5.46
) that is computed using
\mathcal{L}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
given by (
4.2
).
Consider the matrix entry
(2,1)
of (
5.46
). It reads:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k_{+}(q+q^{-1})}\delta_{w}(\cal G_{-}(u))+\frac{k_{-}(q+q^{-1})}{q-q^{-1}}\delta_{w}(1)=

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\frac{f(u)}{k_{+}(q+q^{-1})}\Bigg[\frac{k_{+}}{k_{-}}(q-q^{-1})^{2}{\cal G}_{+}(u)\otimes E^{2}-{\cal G}_{-}(u)\otimes(w^{-2}K^{-1}+w^{2}K)+U(q+q^{-1}){\cal G}_{-}(u)\otimes 1

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt+(q+q^{-1})(q^{2}-q^{-2})\!\left(k_{+}wq^{\frac{1}{2}}(U{\cal W}_{+}(u)\!-\!{\cal W}_{-}(u))\otimes EK^{\frac{1}{2}}\right.+\left.\frac{k_{+}}{wq^{\frac{1}{2}}}(U{\cal W}_{-}(u)\!-\!{\cal W}_{+}(u))\otimes EK^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt+\frac{k_{+}k_{-}(q+q^{-1})^{2}}{(q-q^{-1})}1\otimes\left(\frac{k_{+}}{k_{-}}(q-q^{-1})^{2}E^{2}-(w^{-2}K^{-1}+w^{2}K)\right)\Bigg]+\frac{k_{-}(q+q^{-1})^{2}}{q-q^{-1}}Uf(u)(1\otimes 1)\ .

By definition
\delta_{w}(1)=1\otimes 1
, so the above equation fixes
f(u)=U^{-1}/(q+q^{-1})
, and then
\delta_{w}(\mathcal{G}_{-}(u))
is also fixed. The same result for
f(u)
follows from the matrix entry
(1,2)
. From the matrix entries
(1,1)
and
(2,2)
of (
5.46
) one finds

\displaystyle\delta_{w}(\mathcal{W}_{+}(u))

\displaystyle=f(u)\Big[{\cal W}_{-}(u)\otimes\left((q-q^{-1})^{2}EF-q(K-K^{-1})\right)-(w^{2}+w^{-2}){\cal W}_{+}(u)\otimes 1

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!+\frac{(q-q^{-1})}{k_{+}k_{-}(q+q^{-1})}\left(k_{+}q^{\frac{1}{2}}{\cal G}_{+}(u)\otimes(w^{-1}EK^{\frac{1}{2}})+k_{-}q^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\cal G}_{-}(u)\otimes(wFK^{\frac{1}{2}})\right)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!+(q+q^{-1})\left(1\otimes(k_{+}q^{\frac{1}{2}}w^{-1}EK^{\frac{1}{2}})+1\otimes(k_{-}q^{-\frac{1}{2}}wFK^{\frac{1}{2}})\right)+U(q+q^{-1}){\cal W}_{+}(u)\otimes K\Big]\ .

Then, inserting the power series (
5.1
) and (
5.2
), one gets

\delta_{w}({{\mathsf{W}}}_{0})=\underbrace{f(u)U(q+q^{-1})}_{=1}\Big[1\otimes(k_{+}q^{\frac{1}{2}}w^{-1}EK^{\frac{1}{2}}+k_{-}q^{-\frac{1}{2}}wFK^{\frac{1}{2}})+{{\mathsf{W}}}_{0}\otimes K\Big]\ .

This corresponds to the evaluation of the coaction
\delta({\mathsf{W}}_{0})
given in (
2.44
). Similarly, from the analysis of
\delta_{w}(\mathcal{W}_{-}(u))
, we obtain the evaluation of
\delta({\mathsf{W}}_{1})
given in (
2.45
).
∎

The evaluated coaction of the generating functions (
5.1
), (
5.2
), is readily extracted from (
5.45
):

\displaystyle\delta_{w}(\cal W_{\pm}(u))

\displaystyle=\frac{U^{-1}}{q+q^{-1}}{\cal W}_{\mp}(u)\otimes\left((q-q^{-1})^{2}S_{\pm}S_{\mp}-q(K^{\pm 1}-K^{\mp 1})\right)-\frac{U^{-1}}{q+q^{-1}}(w^{2}+w^{-2}){\cal W}_{\pm}(u)\otimes 1

(5.47)

\displaystyle+\frac{(q-q^{-1})U^{-1}}{k_{+}k_{-}(q+q^{-1})^{2}}\left(k_{+}q^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}{\cal G}_{+}(u)\otimes(w^{\mp 1}S_{+}K^{\pm\frac{1}{2}})+k_{-}q^{\mp\frac{1}{2}}{\cal G}_{-}(u)\otimes(w^{\pm 1}S_{-}K^{\pm\frac{1}{2}})\right)

\displaystyle+U^{-1}\left(1\otimes(k_{+}q^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}w^{\mp 1}S_{+}K^{\pm\frac{1}{2}})+1\otimes(k_{-}q^{\mp\frac{1}{2}}w^{\pm 1}S_{-}K^{\pm\frac{1}{2}})\right)+{\cal W}_{\pm}(u)\otimes K^{\pm 1},

\displaystyle\delta_{w}(\cal G_{\pm}(u))

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{\mp}}{k_{\pm}}\frac{(q-q^{-1})^{2}}{(q+q^{-1})}U^{-1}{\cal G}_{\mp}(u)\otimes S_{\mp}^{2}-\frac{U^{-1}}{q+q^{-1}}{\cal G}_{\pm}(u)\otimes(w^{-2}K^{\pm 1}+w^{2}K^{\mp 1})+{\cal G}_{\pm}(u)\otimes 1

(5.48)

\displaystyle+(q^{2}-q^{-2})\left(k_{\mp}q^{\mp\frac{1}{2}}({\cal W}_{+}(u)-U^{-1}{\cal W}_{-}(u))\otimes(w^{\mp 1}S_{\mp}K^{\frac{1}{2}})\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\left.k_{\mp}q^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}({\cal W}_{-}(u)-U^{-1}{\cal W}_{+}(u))\otimes(w^{\pm 1}S_{\mp}K^{-\frac{1}{2}})\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{k_{+}k_{-}(q+q^{-1})U^{-1}}{(q-q^{-1})}1\otimes\left(\frac{k_{\mp}}{k_{\pm}}(q-q^{-1})^{2}S_{\mp}^{2}-(w^{-2}K^{\pm 1}+w^{2}K^{\mp 1})\right)\ ,

where we used the shorthand notation
S_{+}\equiv E
,
S_{-}\equiv F
. We note that these expressions were first obtained in
[
BS09
, Prop. 2.2]
16
16

16

We corrected typos in
[
BS09
]
, a prefactor was missing.
. Expanding (
5.47
), (
5.48
) as power series in

U^{-1}
, it is straightforward to prove Proposition
2.15
.

Now, using (
5.47
), (
5.48
) we can compute the evaluated coaction of the quantum determinant
\Gamma(u)
from (

):

(5.49)

\delta_{w}(\Gamma(u))=\frac{1}{(u^{2}q+u^{-2}q^{-1})(u^{2}q^{3}+u^{-2}q^{-3})}\Gamma(u)\otimes\gamma(u/w)\gamma(uw)\ ,

where

in Lemma

and the PBW basis of

.

The following result is a natural generalization of Lemma
5.11
.

Proposition 5.12
.

The evaluated coaction of
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)
for
j\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{N}_{+}
is given by

(5.50)

(\delta_{w}\otimes{\text{id}})({\mathcal{K}}^{(j)}(u))=\left(\displaystyle{\prod_{p=1}^{2j}}\frac{U^{-1}}{q+q^{-1}}\big\rvert_{u=uq^{j+\frac{1}{2}-p}}\right)\times\left({\cal L}^{(j)}(u/w)\right)_{[\mathsf{2}]}\left({\mathcal{K}}^{(j)}(u)\right)_{[\mathsf{1}]}\left({\cal L}^{(j)}(uw)\right)_{[\mathsf{2}]}\ .

Proof.

An induction argument is used. For
j=\frac{1}{2}
, the relation (
5.50
) coincides with (
5.45
). Now, suppose
j\geq 1
. For convenience, we omit the notation
[\mathsf{1}]
,
[\mathsf{2}]
. Expand the l.h.s. of (
5.50
) using the expression of the fused K-operator (
5.29
) and the evaluated coaction (
5.50
). It follows:

\displaystyle\delta_{w}({\mathcal{K}}^{(j)}(u))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{(j)}_{\langle 12\rangle}[\delta_{w}({\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{-j+\frac{1}{2}}))]R^{(\frac{1}{2},j-\frac{1}{2})}_{12}(u^{2}q^{-j+1})[\delta_{w}({\mathcal{K}}_{2}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}}))]\mathcal{E}^{(j)}_{\langle 12\rangle}

\displaystyle=\left(\displaystyle{\prod_{p=1}^{2j}}\frac{U^{-1}}{q+q^{-1}}\big\rvert_{u=uq^{j+\frac{1}{2}-p}}\right)\mathcal{F}^{(j)}_{\langle 12\rangle}\mathcal{L}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uw^{-1}q^{-j+\frac{1}{2}}){\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{-j+\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{L}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uwq^{-j+\frac{1}{2}})

(5.51)

\displaystyle\times R_{12}^{(\frac{1}{2},j-\frac{1}{2})}(u^{2}q^{-j+1})\mathcal{L}_{2}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(uw^{-1}q^{\frac{1}{2}}){\mathcal{K}}_{2}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{L}_{2}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(uwq^{\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{E}^{(j)}_{\langle 12\rangle}\ ,

where we used
\delta_{w}
instead of
\delta_{w}\otimes{\text{id}}
for convenience. Multiplying on the left and on the right (
4.50
) by
(\mathcal{L}_{2}^{(j)}(v))^{-1}
and using (
4.54
), we get

(5.52)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)R^{(\frac{1}{2},j-\frac{1}{2})}(u/v)\mathcal{L}_{2}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(v^{-1})=\mathcal{L}_{2}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(v^{-1})R^{(\frac{1}{2},j-\frac{1}{2})}(u/v)\mathcal{L}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\ .

Using (
5.52
) for
u\rightarrow uwq^{-j+\frac{1}{2}},v\rightarrow u^{-1}wq^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and the commutation
[{\mathcal{K}}^{(j_{1})}_{1}(u),\mathcal{L}^{(j_{2})}_{2}(v)]=0
, (
5.51
) becomes:

(5.53)

\displaystyle=\left(\displaystyle{\prod_{p=1}^{2j}}\frac{U^{-1}}{q+q^{-1}}\big\rvert_{u=uq^{j+\frac{1}{2}-p}}\right)\mathcal{F}^{(j)}_{\langle 12\rangle}\mathcal{L}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uw^{-1}q^{-j+\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{L}_{2}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(uw^{-1}q^{\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle\times{\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{-j+\frac{1}{2}})R_{12}^{(\frac{1}{2},j-\frac{1}{2})}(u^{2}q^{-j+1}){\mathcal{K}}_{2}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{L}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uwq^{-j+\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{L}_{2}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(uwq^{\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{E}^{(j)}_{\langle 12\rangle}\ .

On the other hand, inserting (
4.51
), (
5.29
), in the r.h.s. of (
5.50
) one gets:

\displaystyle\delta_{w}({\mathcal{K}}^{(j)}(u))

\displaystyle=\left(\displaystyle{\prod_{p=1}^{2j}}\frac{U^{-1}}{q+q^{-1}}\big\rvert_{u=uq^{j+\frac{1}{2}-p}}\right)\mathcal{F}^{(j)}_{\langle 12\rangle}\mathcal{L}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uw^{-1}q^{-j+\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{L}_{2}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(uw^{-1}q^{\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{E}^{(j)}_{\langle 12\rangle}\mathcal{F}^{(j)}_{\langle 12\rangle}

\displaystyle\times{\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{-j+\frac{1}{2}})R_{12}^{(\frac{1}{2},j-\frac{1}{2})}(u^{2}q^{-j+1}){\mathcal{K}}_{2}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{E}^{(j)}_{\langle 12\rangle}\mathcal{F}^{(j)}_{\langle 12\rangle}\mathcal{L}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uwq^{-j+\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{L}_{2}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(uwq^{\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{E}^{(j)}_{\langle 12\rangle}\ .

Now, in order to remove the products
\mathcal{E}^{(j)}_{\langle 12\rangle}\mathcal{F}^{(j)}_{\langle 12\rangle}
, multiply first the expression above from the right by
\mathcal{H}^{(j)}_{\langle 12\rangle}[\mathcal{H}^{(j)}_{\langle 12\rangle}]^{-1}
. Then, using the relations (
3.48
)-(
3.50
), (
D.5
) and

(5.54)

R^{(\frac{1}{2},j-\frac{1}{2})}(v/u)\mathcal{L}_{2}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(v)\mathcal{L}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)=\mathcal{L}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\mathcal{L}_{2}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(v)R^{(\frac{1}{2},j-\frac{1}{2})}(v/u)

which is obtained from the RLL equation, after simplifications the expression matches with (
5.53
).
∎

5.6.
Twisted intertwining relations for fused K-operators

In the next section, we will also need the twisted intertwining relations satisfied by the fused K-operators. For the fundamental K-operator (
5.4
), the twisted intertwining relations have been given in
[
BS09
, Prop. 4.2]
. This result is now extended to higher values of
j
.

Proposition 5.13
.

The following relation holds for any
j\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{N}_{+}
and all
b\in\mathcal{A}_{q}
:

(5.55)

{\cal K}^{(j)}(v)({\text{id}}\otimes\pi^{j})[\delta_{v^{-1}}(b)]=({\text{id}}\otimes\pi^{j})[\delta_{v}(b)]{\cal K}^{(j)}(v)\ .

Proof.

Lemma
5.11
implies that

(5.56)

({\text{id}}\otimes\pi^{j}\otimes{\text{id}})[(\delta_{v}\otimes{\text{id}})({\cal K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u))]=\frac{U^{-1}}{q+q^{-1}}R^{(j,\frac{1}{2})}(u/v){\cal K}_{2}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)R^{(j,\frac{1}{2})}(uv)\ .

We then notice that
{\cal K}^{(j)}(v)
satisfies the equation

(5.57)

{\cal K}^{(j)}_{1}(v)R^{(j,\frac{1}{2})}(uv){\cal K}_{2}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)R^{(j,\frac{1}{2})}(u/v)=R^{(j,\frac{1}{2})}(u/v){\cal K}_{2}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)R^{(j,\frac{1}{2})}(uv){\cal K}^{(j)}_{1}(v)\ .

This version of reflection equation follows from the standard reflection equation (
5.30
) for
j_{1}=j,j_{2}=\frac{1}{2}
and
u_{1}=v,u_{2}=u
. Indeed, we multiply (
5.30
) on the left and the right by
R^{(j_{1},j_{2})}(u_{2}/u_{1})
and using (
4.55
) we obtain (
5.57
).

Now using (
5.56
), the equation (
5.57
) can be rewritten as

(5.58)

{\cal K}_{1}^{(j)}(v)({\text{id}}\otimes\pi^{j}\otimes{\text{id}})[(\delta_{v^{-1}}\otimes{\text{id}})({\cal K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u))]=({\text{id}}\otimes\pi^{j}\otimes{\text{id}})[(\delta_{v}\otimes{\text{id}})({\cal K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u))]{\cal K}_{1}^{(j)}(v)\ .

This equation can be thought as an equation in
\mathcal{A}_{q}[[u^{-1}]]((v^{-1}))\otimes\text{\rm End}(\mathbb{C}^{2j+1})\otimes\text{\rm End}(\mathbb{C}^{2})
.
Denote the entries of the fundamental K-operator by
{\cal K}_{mn}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\in{\cal A}_{q}[[u^{-1}]]
,
m,n=1,2
. Now, considering (
5.58
) as four equations in
\mathcal{A}_{q}[[u^{-1}]]((v^{-1}))\otimes\text{\rm End}(\mathbb{C}^{2j+1})
, i.e. taking the matrix elements of
\text{\rm End}(\mathbb{C}^{2})
, it yields

\displaystyle{\cal K}^{(j)}(v)\left(({\text{id}}\otimes\pi^{j})[\delta_{v^{-1}}({\cal K}_{mn}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u))]\right)=\left(({\text{id}}\otimes\pi^{j})[\delta_{v}({\cal K}_{mn}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u))]\right){\cal K}^{(j)}(v)\ .

Inserting the entries according to (
5.4
), extracting the independent relations and using (
5.1
), (
5.2
), this implies (
5.55
).
∎

6.
Fused K-operators and the universal K-matrix for
{\cal A}_{q}

In this section, we assume there exists a universal K-matrix
\mathfrak{K}
for
{\cal A}_{q}
. We are interested in the precise relationship between the fused K-operators
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)
constructed in the previous section using (
5.29
) and the spin-
j
K-operators defined in (
4.56
) through the evaluation of the universal K-matrix.
Motivated by Lemma
4.9
relating spin-
j
L-operators (
4.1
) and fused L-operators (
4.27
), and by supporting evidences discussed below, we propose the following conjecture.

Conjecture 1
.

For
j\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{N}
, we have

(6.1)

{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)=\nu^{(j)}(u){\cal K}^{(j)}(u)\quad\in{\cal A}_{q}[[u^{-1}]]\otimes\text{\rm End}(\mathbb{C}^{2j+1})\ ,

where
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)
is defined in (
5.29
) with

(6.2)

\displaystyle\nu^{(j)}(u)

\displaystyle=\displaystyle{\left(\prod_{m=0}^{2j-1}\nu(uq^{j-\frac{1}{2}-m})\right)}\left(\displaystyle\prod_{k=0}^{2j-2}\prod_{\ell=0}^{2j-k-2}\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mu(u^{2}q^{2j-2-2k-\ell}))\right)\ .

Here
\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mu(u))
is given by (
4.9
) and
\nu(u)\equiv\nu^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
is an invertible central element in
{\cal A}_{q}[[u^{-1}]]
,
defined by the functional relation

(6.3)

\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mu(u^{2}q))\nu(u)\nu(uq)\Gamma(u)=1\ ,

where
\Gamma(u)
is given in (
5.20
),
and has the evaluated coaction

(6.4)

\delta_{w}(\nu(u))=(u^{2}q+u^{-2}q^{-1})\nu(u)\otimes\mu(u/w)\mu(uw)\ .

We note that a solution
\nu(u)
to (
6.3
) can be uniquely (up to a sign of its constant term) determined by recursion provided in the next lemma. Here, we assume that
\nu(u)
is invertible and generates a commutative subalgebra in
{\cal A}_{q}((u^{-1}))
.
In what follows we use the following notation

(6.5)

F(u):=\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mu(u^{2}q))\Gamma(u)\ .

We observe that only even modes of
F(u)
are non-zero, i.e.

(6.6)

\displaystyle F(u)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}F_{k}u^{-2k}\ .

Recall the central elements from Example
5.3
, in terms of which the first modes of
F(u)
read:

(6.7)

\displaystyle F_{0}=-\frac{\rho q^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(q-q^{-1})^{2}}\ ,\quad F_{1}=\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}}c_{1}}{2(q-q^{-1})}\Delta_{1}\ ,\quad F_{2}=\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}}c_{2}}{2(q-q^{-1})}\Delta_{2}-\frac{\rho q^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(q^{4}-1)}\ ,

with
c_{k}
defined in (
5.23
).

Lemma 6.1
.

The generating function
\nu(u)=\nu^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
from (
6.1
) is central in
{\cal A}_{q}[[u^{-1}]]
and takes the following form

(6.8)

\displaystyle\nu(u)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\nu_{k}u^{-2k}

where
\nu_{0}
is fixed up to a sign by

(6.9)

\nu_{0}^{2}=-\rho^{-1}q^{-\frac{1}{2}}(q-q^{-1})^{2}\ ,

and all
\nu_{k}
with
k\geq 1
are determined recursively by

(6.10)

\displaystyle\nu_{k}=-\frac{1}{F_{0}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}\frac{1-q^{-2(k+\ell)}}{1-q^{-4k}}F_{k-\ell}\nu_{\ell}\ .

Proof.

With the notation (
6.5
), the functional relation (
6.3
) takes the form

(6.11)

F(u)\nu(u)\nu(uq)=1\ .

Replacing
u\to u{q^{-1}}
, we get from it another equation

(6.12)

F(uq^{-1})\nu(uq^{-1})\nu(u)=1\ .

Comparing both the equations (
6.11
) and (
6.12
), and using that by assumption
[\nu(u),\nu(uq^{\pm 1})]=0
and
\nu(u)
is invertible, we get

(6.13)

\displaystyle F(uq)\nu(uq^{2})=F(u)\nu(u)\ .

Assuming
\nu(u)\in{\cal A}_{q}((u^{-1}))
with an expansion
\nu(u)=\sum_{k=-M}^{\infty}\nu^{\prime}_{k}u^{-k}
for some positive
M
and using (
6.13
) with (
6.6
) we conclude by induction that
M
has to be zero.
We can then assume that
\nu(u)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\nu^{\prime}_{k}u^{-k}
. Inserting (
6.6
) in the above equation (
6.13
), it is equivalent to

\displaystyle\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}u^{-p}\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}\rfloor}(q^{-2p+2k}-1)F_{k}\nu^{\prime}_{p-2k}=0\ \quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}\rfloor}(q^{-2p+2k}-1)F_{k}\nu^{\prime}_{p-2k}=0\ ,\quad p=0,1,2,...

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. For
p=2n+1
, one gets

(6.14)

\displaystyle\nu^{\prime}_{2n+1}=-\frac{1}{F_{0}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\frac{1-q^{-2(2n+1)+2k}}{1-q^{-2(2n+1)}}\right)F_{k}\nu^{\prime}_{2n+1-2k}\ .

For
n=0
, it gives
\nu^{\prime}_{1}=0
which implies by induction that all modes
\nu^{\prime}_{2n+1}=0
.
Thus, the power series
\nu(u)
is of the form (
6.8
). From (
6.11
) we readily see that
\nu_{0}
is fixed as in (
6.9
). Then, the recurrence relation that follows from (
6.13
) with (
6.8
) and (
6.6
) is now given by

\sum_{l=0}^{p}(q^{-2(p+l)}-1)F_{p-l}\,\nu_{l}=0\ ,

which determines
\nu_{l}
as in (
6.10
). ∎

Example 6.2
.

Together with (
6.7
) and (
5.24
) and (
5.25
), we get explicit expression of the first few modes of
\nu(u)
developed in (
6.8
) in terms of the alternating generators of
{\cal A}_{q}
:

(6.15)

\nu_{1}=\frac{(q-q^{-1})^{2}q^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu_{0}}{\rho(q+q^{-1})}F_{1}\ ,\qquad\nu_{2}=\frac{(q-q^{-1})^{2}q^{3/2}\nu_{0}}{\rho(q^{2}+q^{-2})}\left(F_{2}+q^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{(q-q^{-1})(q^{3}-q^{-3})}{\rho(q+q^{-1})^{2}}F_{1}^{2}\right)\ ,

where
\nu_{0}
is fixed as a square root of (
6.9
).

Let us now show that
{\mathbf{K}}^{(j)}(u)\in\mathcal{A}_{q}[[u^{-1}]]\otimes\text{\rm End}(\mathbb{C}^{2j+1})
.
First, we note that the leading term of
\nu^{(j)}(u)
given in (
6.2
) is at
u^{-4j^{2}+2j}
, using (
6.8
) and (
4.9
). Next, from the definition of
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)
in (
5.29
), we find that its leading term is at
u^{4j^{2}-2j}
, using the fact that the leading terms of
\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
and
R^{(\frac{1}{2},j)}(u)
from (
3.41
) are respectively at
u^{0}
and
u^{2j}
. Finally, we conclude that
\nu^{(j)}(u)\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)
is in
\mathcal{A}_{q}[[u^{-1}]]\otimes\text{\rm End}(\mathbb{C}^{2j+1})
with its leading term at
u^{0}
.

Below, we present supporting evidences for Conjecture
1
, and then we derive from this conjecture certain properties of the fused K-operators for
j\geq 0
.

6.1.
Supporting evidences

For the clarity of the presentation, let us define:

(6.16)

\tilde{{\bf K}}^{(j)}(u)=\nu^{(j)}(u){\cal K}^{(j)}(u)\ \quad\mbox{for\ \ $j\in\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{N}}$}\ ,

where we assume
\nu^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
is an invertible central element in
{\cal A}_{q}[[u^{-1}]]
.
Importantly, it is not assumed that
\tilde{{\bf K}}^{(j)}(u)
is obtained from the evaluation of a universal K-matrix.

We provide supporting evidences for Conjecture
1
.
We show that
\tilde{{\bf K}}^{(j)}(u)
for all
j
satisfy the following systems of equations:

(K1’)

\displaystyle\tilde{{\bf K}}^{(j)}(v)({\text{id}}\otimes\pi^{j})[\delta_{v^{-1}}(b)]

\displaystyle=({\text{id}}\otimes\pi^{j})[\delta_{v}(b)]\tilde{{\bf K}}^{(j)}(v)\ ,

(K2’)

\displaystyle(\delta_{w}\otimes{\text{id}})(\tilde{{\bf K}}^{(j)}(u))=\left({\bf L}^{(j)}(u/w)\right)_{\textsf{[2]}}

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{{\bf K}}^{(j)}(u)\right)_{\textsf{[1]}}\left({\bf L}^{(j)}(uw)\right)_{\textsf{[2]}}\ ,

(K3’)

\displaystyle\tilde{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)=\mathcal{F}^{(j)}_{\langle 12\rangle}\tilde{\bf K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{-j+\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{(\frac{1}{2},j-\frac{1}{2})}(u^{2}q^{-j+1})\tilde{\bf K}_{2}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\mathcal{E}^{(j)}_{\langle 12\rangle}\ ,

where
\mathcal{R}^{(\frac{1}{2},j)}(u)
is given in (
4.18
), if and only if (
6.2
) and (
6.4
) hold.
Here, (
K1’
), (
K2’
), (
K3’
) are direct analogs of (
4.84
), (
4.83
), (
4.72
), respectively.
We will also show that
\tilde{{\bf K}}^{(j)}(u)
satisfies the reflection equation (
4.67
) where
{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)
is replaced by
\tilde{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)
.

6.1.1.
Fusion relation (
K3’
), twisted intertwining relations (
K1’
) and evaluated coaction (
K2’
)

We assume that
\nu(u)
is an invertible central element in
{\cal A}_{q}[[u^{-1}]]
.

Lemma 6.3
.

The K-operators

\tilde{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)
for all
j\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{N}
satisfy the fusion relation (
K3’
)
if and only if
\nu^{(j)}(u)
takes the form (
6.2
).

Proof.

Note first that (
5.29
) contains
R^{(\frac{1}{2},j)}(u)
while (
K3’
) contains
\mathcal{R}^{(\frac{1}{2},j)}(u)
, and they are related by

(6.17)

\mathcal{R}^{(\frac{1}{2},j)}(u)=\left(\prod_{k=0}^{2j-1}\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\mu(uq^{-j+\frac{1}{2}+k})\big)\right)R^{(\frac{1}{2},j)}(u)\

due to (
4.41
) and (
4.29
).
Then, inserting (
6.16
) in (
K3’
) and using (
5.29
) and (
6.17
),
the assumption that
\nu(u)
is an invertible central element in
\mathcal{A}_{q}[[u^{-1}]]
, and that
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)
is invertible, see Remark
5.9
, the resulting recursion relation on
\nu^{(j)}(u)
is equivalent to (
6.2
).
∎

In what follows, we will assume that (
K3’
) holds, so in particular all
\nu^{(j)}(u)
are central. Then, using Proposition
5.13
, the twisted intertwining relation for
\tilde{{\bf K}}^{(j)}(u)
is immediate:

Lemma 6.4
.

The K-operators
\tilde{\mathbf{K}}^{(j)}(u)
for all
j\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{N}
satisfy (
K1’
).

We now show that the evaluated coaction (
K2’
) holds for
\tilde{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)
.

Lemma 6.5
.

The K-operators
\tilde{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)
for all
j\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{N}
satisfy (
K2’
)
if and only if
\delta_{w}(\nu(u))
is given by (
6.4
).

Proof.

We first consider the case
j=\frac{1}{2}
. Inserting (
6.16
) in (
K2’
) for
j=\frac{1}{2}
, using (
5.45
), and the invertibility of
\mathcal{L}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
,
\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
,
the resulting equation is equivalent to (
6.4
).
It remains to check that given the evaluated coaction (
6.4
), (
K2’
) holds for higher
j
. Now, consider
j\geq 1
.
On one hand, inserting (
6.16
) in (
K2’
), the l.h.s. reads

(6.18)

\displaystyle\delta_{w}\big(\nu^{(j)}(u)\big)\delta_{w}(\mathcal{K}^{(j)}

\displaystyle(u))=\displaystyle{\left(\prod_{m=0}^{2j-1}\delta_{w}(\nu(uq^{j-\frac{1}{2}-m}))\right)}\!\left(\displaystyle\prod_{k=0}^{2j-2}\prod_{\ell=0}^{2j-k-2}\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mu(u^{2}q^{2j-2-2k-\ell}))\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\displaystyle{\prod_{p=1}^{2j}}\frac{U^{-1}}{q+q^{-1}}\big\rvert_{u=uq^{j+\frac{1}{2}-p}}\right)\!\!\left({\cal L}^{(j)}(u/w)\right)_{[\mathsf{2}]}\!\left({\mathcal{K}}^{(j)}(u)\right)_{[\mathsf{1}]}\!\left({\cal L}^{(j)}(uw)\right)_{[\mathsf{2}]}\ ,

where we used (
5.50
), (
6.2
) and the fact that
\delta_{w}
in an algebra homomorphism. On the other hand, the r.h.s. of (
K2’
) is

(6.19)

\nu^{(j)}(u)\otimes\mu^{(j)}(u/w)\mu^{(j)}(uw)\left({\cal L}^{(j)}(u/w)\right)_{[\mathsf{2}]}\left({\mathcal{K}}^{(j)}(u)\right)_{[\mathsf{1}]}\left({\cal L}^{(j)}(uw)\right)_{[\mathsf{2}]}\ .

Then, replacing
\mu^{(j)}(u)
and
\nu^{(j)}(u)
in (
6.19
) by (
4.29
), (
6.2
) respectively, and using (
6.4
) in (
6.18
), we find that (
6.18
) and (
6.19
) are equal.
∎

Finally, assuming (
K3’
) holds, so that
\nu^{(j)}(u)
are central by Lemma
6.3
, we show that the fused K-operators
\tilde{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)
satisfy the reflection equation.

Lemma 6.6
.

The K-operators
\tilde{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)
satisfy the reflection equation (
4.67
) where
{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)
is replaced by
\tilde{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)
for any
j_{1}
,
j_{2}\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{N}_{+}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.7
, the fused K-operators
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}(u)
satisfy the equation (
5.30
). Then, multiplying this equation by
\nu^{(j_{1})}(u)\nu^{(j_{2})}(v)
and using the fact that they are central, we obtain (
4.67
) where
{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)
is replaced by
\tilde{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)
.
∎

6.1.2.
Functional relation on
\nu(u)

We have seen in Lemma
6.3
that the relation (
K3’
) fixes the normalization factor
\nu^{(j)}(u)
as (
6.2
). Here we show that the analog of the reduction relation (
4.73
) for
\tilde{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)

leads to the functional relation (
6.3
).
Recall the functional relation on
\mu(u)
in (
4.30
) was obtained by comparing the fusion relation with the reduction relation satisfied by the spin-
j
L-operators, see Proposition
4.10
. We proceed similarly for
\tilde{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)
.

Proposition 6.7
.

The K-operators
\tilde{\bf K}^{(j)}(u)
satisfy (
K3’
) and

(K3”)

\tilde{\bf K}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(u)=\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{\langle 12\rangle}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}\tilde{\bf K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{j+1})\mathcal{R}^{(\frac{1}{2},j)}(u^{2}q^{j+\frac{3}{2}})\tilde{\bf K}_{2}^{(j)}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\langle 12\rangle}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}\ ,

for
j=1
if and only if
\nu(u)
satisfies the functional relation (
6.3
).

Proof.

The equation (
K3”
) for
j=1
in terms of fused K-operators reads as

(6.20)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\!=\!\nu(uq)\nu(uq^{2})\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mu(u^{2}q)\mu(u^{2}q^{2})\mu(u^{2}q^{3}))\bar{\mathcal{F}}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{\langle 12\rangle}\mathcal{K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{2})R^{(\frac{1}{2},1)}(u^{2}q^{\frac{5}{2}})\mathcal{K}_{2}^{(1)}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\langle 12\rangle}^{(\frac{1}{2})}\ ,

where we used
\tilde{{\bf K}}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)=\nu(u){\cal K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
, the factorized form (
6.2
) for
\nu^{(1)}(u)
due to Lemma
6.3
and we used
\mathcal{R}^{(\frac{1}{2},1)}(u)=\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mu(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\mu(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}}))R^{(\frac{1}{2},1)}(u)
, recall (
6.17
). From the relation satisfied by
\mu(u)
given in (
4.30
) and using
\pi^{j}(C)=q^{2j+1}+q^{-2j-1}
, one gets:

(6.21)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mu(u)\mu(uq))=\frac{1}{c(u)c(uq^{2})}\ ,

where
c(u)
is given in (
3.42
) and by our standard convention the right hand side is developed as power series in
u^{-1}
. Then, the equation (
6.20
) becomes

(6.22)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)

\displaystyle=\frac{\nu(uq)\nu(uq^{2})\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mu(u^{2}q^{3}))}{c(u^{2}q)c(u^{2}q^{3})}\bar{\mathcal{F}}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{\langle 12\rangle}\mathcal{K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{2})R^{(\frac{1}{2},1)}(u^{2}q^{\frac{5}{2}})\mathcal{K}_{2}^{(1)}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\langle 12\rangle}^{(\frac{1}{2})}\ .

The r.h.s. of (
6.22
) is now computed using the expressions for
\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
,
\mathcal{K}^{(1)}(u)
given respectively in (
5.4
), (
5.37
), the fused R-matrix (
5.35
) and
\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\langle 12\rangle}^{(\frac{1}{2})}
,
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{\langle 12\rangle}^{(\frac{1}{2})}
given by:

\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\langle 12\rangle}^{(\frac{1}{2})}=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
1&0\\
0&\sqrt{[2]_{q}}\\
-\sqrt{[2]_{q}}&0\\
0&-1\\
0&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{\langle 12\rangle}^{(\frac{1}{2})}=\begin{pmatrix}0&\frac{1}{1+[2]_{q}}&0&-\frac{\sqrt{[2]_{q}}}{1+[2]_{q}}&0&0\\
0&0&\frac{\sqrt{[2]_{q}}}{1+[2]_{q}}&0&-\frac{1}{1+[2]_{q}}&0\end{pmatrix}.

In terms of the quantum determinant (
5.20
), one finds:

(6.23)

\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{{\langle 12\rangle}}^{(\frac{1}{2})}{\cal K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{2})R^{\left(\frac{1}{2},1\right)}(u^{2}q^{\frac{5}{2}}){\cal K}_{2}^{(1)}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\langle 12\rangle}^{(\frac{1}{2})}=c(u^{2}q)c(u^{2}q^{3})\Gamma(uq){\cal K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\ .

This relation is obtained by applying the ordering relations for
\mathcal{A}_{q}
given in Appendix
C
.
Inserting this expression in (
6.22
) and multiplying by
[{\cal K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)]^{-1}
given in (
5.27
) the relation (
6.3
) follows.
∎

Remark 6.8
.

As a consistency check, we observe the following:

(1)

The evaluated coaction in (
6.4
) respects the functional relation (
6.3
) on
\nu(u)
. Indeed, using (
5.49
) and the functional relations (
4.30
) and (
6.3
), we obtain

\delta_{w}(\nu(u))\delta_{w}(\nu(uq))\delta_{w}(\Gamma(u))\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mu(u^{2}q))=1\otimes 1\ .

(2)

The expression for
\delta_{w}(\nu(u))
in (
6.4
) agrees with the direct calculation of
\delta_{w}(\nu_{1})
.
To see it, we first recall the expression of
\nu_{1}
in (
6.15
) together with (
6.7
) and (
5.24
). With the expressions from Proposition
2.15
, we need to calculate only
the evaluated coaction on
\tilde{{\mathsf{G}}}_{1}
. This is the 1st mode of the current
\cal G_{-}(u)
, recall (
5.2
), and thus
\delta_{w}(\tilde{{\mathsf{G}}}_{1})
is extracted from the 1st mode of (
5.48
):

\displaystyle\delta_{w}({\tilde{{\mathsf{G}}}}_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{+}k_{-}(q+q^{-1})}{q-q^{-1}}1\otimes\big(\frac{k_{+}}{k_{-}}(q-q^{-1})^{2}E^{2}-(w^{-2}K^{-1}+w^{2}K)\big)\big)

\displaystyle+{\tilde{\mathsf{G}}}_{1}\otimes 1+(q^{2}-q^{-2})k_{+}(q^{\frac{1}{2}}{{\mathsf{W}}}_{0}\otimes(wEK^{\frac{1}{2}})+q^{-\frac{1}{2}}{{\mathsf{W}}}_{1}\otimes(w^{-1}EK^{-\frac{1}{2}}))\ .

Combining all these expressions of
\delta_{w}
together as in (
5.24
), using that
\delta_{w}
is an algebra map for any
w
, and simplifying we obtain

\delta_{w}(\Delta_{1})=\Delta_{1}\otimes 1-\frac{2k_{+}k_{-}(w^{2}+w^{-2})}{q-q^{-1}}1\otimes C\ ,

where
C
is the Casimir element (
A.5
).
Using (
6.15
) together with the expression of
F_{1}
in (
6.7
), we get the final result

\delta_{w}(\nu_{1})=\nu_{1}\otimes 1+\frac{q^{-1}\nu_{0}}{q+q^{-1}}(w^{2}+w^{-2})\otimes C

which indeed agrees with the term at
u^{-2}
in the expansion of (
6.4
).

6.2.
Coaction

Motivated by the discussion in Section
4.3
and Proposition
4.20
,
we propose a right coaction for the components of
\tilde{{\bf K}}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
:

(6.24)

(\delta\otimes{\text{id}})(\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u))=\left([{\bf L}^{-}(u^{-1})]^{-1}\right)_{[2]}\left(\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\right)_{[1]}\left({\bf L}^{+}(u)\right)_{[2]}\ ,

where
{\bf L}^{\pm}(u)
are defined in (
4.74
) and are calculated in (
B.26
) and (
B.33
), respectively.
First of all, we show that the proposed coaction (
6.24
) indeed respects the relations satisfied by the components of
\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
.
Recall that, due to Lemmas
6.6
and
4.13
, these relations are

(6.25)

R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u/v)\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{1}(u)R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(uv)\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{2}(v)=\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{2}(v)R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(uv)\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{1}(u)R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u/v)\ .

Lemma 6.9
.

The following element belonging to
({\cal A}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{L}U_{q}sl_{2})[[u^{-1}]]\otimes\text{\rm End}(\mathbb{C}^{2})
and defined by

(6.26)

\tilde{\bf K}^{(-,+)}(u)=\left([{\bf L}^{-}(u^{-1})]^{-1}\right)_{[2]}\left(\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\right)_{[1]}\left({\bf L}^{+}(u)\right)_{[2]}

satisfies the reflection equation (
6.25
) where
\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
is replaced by
\tilde{\bf K}^{(-,+)}(u)
.

Proof.

We first substitute the K-operators in (
6.26
) into the reflection equation (
6.25
). Then, we multiply both sides from the left by
{\bf L}_{1}^{-}(u^{-1}){\bf L}_{2}^{-}(v^{-1})
and from the right by
[{\bf L}_{2}^{+}(v)]^{-1}[{\bf L}_{1}^{+}(u)]^{-1}
. One has for the l.h.s. of the resulting equation:

\displaystyle{\bf L}_{1}^{-}(u^{-1})

\displaystyle\underline{{\bf L}_{2}^{-}(v^{-1})R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u/v)[{\bf L}_{1}^{-}(u^{-1})]^{-1}}\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{1}(u){\bf L}_{1}^{+}(u)R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(uv)[{\bf L}^{-}_{2}(v^{-1})]^{-1}\underline{\tilde{\bf K}_{2}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(v)[{\bf L}_{1}^{+}(u)]^{-1}}

\displaystyle=R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u/v)\underline{{\bf L}_{2}^{-}(v^{-1})\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{1}(u)}\ \underline{{\bf L}_{1}^{+}(u)R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(uv)[{\bf L}^{-}_{2}(v^{-1})]^{-1}}[{\bf L}_{1}^{+}(u)]^{-1}\tilde{\bf K}_{2}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(v)

(6.27)

\displaystyle=R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u/v)\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{1}(u)R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(uv)\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{2}(v)\ ,

where we underlined the steps of calculation that correspond either to the commutation relations between L- and K-operators associated with different auxiliary spaces or to the use of variations of (
4.75
), (
4.76
). For instance, in the first line we use

(6.28)

{\bf L}_{2}^{-}(v^{-1})R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u/v)[{\bf L}_{1}^{-}(u^{-1})]^{-1}=[{\bf L}_{1}^{-}(u^{-1})]^{-1}R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u/v){\bf L}_{2}^{-}(v^{-1})\ ,

which is obtained by multiplying the equation (
4.75
), with the all-minus choice, from the left by

[{\bf L}^{-}_{1}(u)]^{-1}R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(v/u)
and from the right by
R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(v/u)[{\bf L}^{-}_{1}(u)]^{-1}
, then using the unitarity property of the R-matrix given in (
4.55
),
and finally, substituting
u\rightarrow u^{-1}
,
v\rightarrow v^{-1}
.

On the other hand, the r.h.s. of the equation obtained in the first paragraph of this proof takes the following form:

\displaystyle\underline{{\bf L}_{1}^{-}(u^{-1})\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{2}(v)}{\bf L}^{+}_{2}(v)R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(uv)[{\bf L}^{-}_{1}(u^{-1})]^{-1}\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{1}(u)\underline{{\bf L}_{1}^{+}(u)R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u/v)[{\bf L}_{2}^{+}(v)]^{-1}}[{\bf L}_{1}^{+}(u)]^{-1}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{2}(v){\bf L}_{1}^{-}(u^{-1})\underline{{\bf L}^{+}_{2}(v)R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(uv)[{\bf L}^{-}_{1}(u^{-1})]^{-1}}\ \underline{\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{1}(u)[{\bf L}_{2}^{+}(v)]^{-1}}R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u/v)

(6.29)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{2}(v)R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(uv)\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{1}(u)R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u/v)\ .

Finally, comparing (
6.27
) with (
6.29
), one gets the reflection equation (
6.25
) that was proven in Lemma
6.6
.
∎

We finally show that
\delta
defined in (
6.24
) is coassociative and counital, see (
2.18
).
Firstly, we check the coassociativity:

\displaystyle(\delta\otimes{\text{id}}\otimes{\text{id}})\circ(\delta\otimes{\text{id}})(\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u))

\displaystyle=(\delta\otimes{\text{id}}\otimes{\text{id}})\left(\left([{\bf L}^{-}(u^{-1})]^{-1}\right)_{[2]}\left(\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\right)_{[1]}\left({\bf L}^{+}(u)\right)_{[2]}\right)

\displaystyle=\left([{\bf L}^{-}(u^{-1})]^{-1}\right)_{[3]}\left([{\bf L}^{-}(u^{-1})]^{-1}\right)_{[2]}\left(\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\right)_{[1]}\left({\bf L}^{+}(u)\right)_{[2]}\left({\bf L}^{+}(u)\right)_{[3]}

\displaystyle=({\text{id}}\otimes\Delta\otimes{\text{id}})\circ(\delta\otimes{\text{id}})(\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u))\ ,

where the coproduct is given in (
4.78
) and we used
(\Delta\otimes{\text{id}})([{\bf L}^{\mp}(u)]^{-1})=\left({\bf L}^{\mp}(u)\right)^{-1}_{[2]}\left({\bf L}^{\mp}(u)\right)^{-1}_{[1]}
.
Secondly, the condition with the counit is checked:

\displaystyle({\text{id}}\otimes\epsilon\otimes{\text{id}})\circ(\delta\otimes{\text{id}})(\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u))

\displaystyle=({\text{id}}\otimes\epsilon\otimes{\text{id}})\circ\left(\left([{\bf L}^{\mp}(u^{-1})]^{-1}\right)_{[2]}\left(\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\right)_{[1]}\left({\bf L}^{\pm}(u)\right)_{[2]}\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\ ,

where we used (
4.80
).

Let us finally check that the coaction proposed in (
6.24
) reproduces the ‘standard’
q
-Onsager coaction (
2.44
)-(
2.45
) for the first two generators
{\mathsf{W}}_{0}
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
. Recall from (
6.16
) that
\tilde{{\bf K}}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)=\nu(u){\cal K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
, with
\nu(u)
calculated in Lemma
6.1
and
{\cal K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
defined in (
5.4
). With the Ding-Frenkel L-operators computed in (
B.26
) and (
B.33
),
we compare leading terms at
u^{-1}
of the matrix entries
(1,1)
and
(2,2)
of both sides of (
6.24
) which gives

(6.30)

\displaystyle\delta({{\mathsf{W}}}_{0})

\displaystyle=1\otimes\left(k_{+}q^{\frac{1}{2}}E_{1}K_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}+k_{-}q^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{1}K_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)+{{\mathsf{W}}}_{0}\otimes K_{1}\ ,

(6.31)

\displaystyle\delta({{\mathsf{W}}}_{1})

\displaystyle=1\otimes\left(k_{+}q^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{0}K_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}+k_{-}q^{\frac{1}{2}}E_{0}K_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)+{{\mathsf{W}}}_{1}\otimes K_{0}\ .

These formulas indeed agree with (
2.44
) and (
2.45
), respectively.

6.3.
Comments

Based on the supporting evidences given in the previous subsection, we believe Conjecture
1
is correct.
Some straightforward consequences are now pointed out. Firstly, some relations among the fused K-operators (
5.29
) are derived. They generalize the relation (
6.23
).

Proposition 6.10
.

Assume Conjecture
1
. Then, the following relations hold for any
j\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{N}_{+}
:

(6.32)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{F}}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}_{\langle 12\rangle}\mathcal{K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{j+1})

\displaystyle R^{(\frac{1}{2},j)}(u^{2}q^{j+\frac{3}{2}})\mathcal{K}^{(j)}_{2}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\langle 12\rangle}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}=

\displaystyle\left(\displaystyle{\prod_{k=0}^{2j-2}}c(u^{2}q^{2j-1-k})c(u^{2}q^{2j+1-k})\right)\Gamma(uq^{j})\mathcal{K}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(u)\ ,

(6.33)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{F}}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}_{\langle 12\rangle}\mathcal{K}_{2}^{(j)}(uq^{-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle R^{(\frac{1}{2},j)}(u^{2}q^{-j-\frac{3}{2}})\mathcal{K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{-j-1})\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\langle 12\rangle}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}=

\displaystyle\left(\displaystyle{\prod_{k=0}^{2j-2}}c(u^{2}q^{-2j+2+k})c(u^{2}q^{-2j+k})\right)\Gamma(uq^{-j-1})\mathcal{K}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(u)\ ,

where
\bar{\mathcal{E}}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}
is fixed by Lemma
3.2
and
\bar{\mathcal{F}}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}
is given in (
3.33
) with (
3.34
).

Proof.

From Remark
3.4
, the intertwining property with
\Delta^{op}
reads:

(6.34)

\bar{\mathcal{E}}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(\pi_{u}^{j-\frac{1}{2}})(x)=(\pi_{uq^{-j-1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\otimes\pi_{uq^{-\frac{1}{2}}}^{j})(\Delta^{op}(x))\bar{\mathcal{E}}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}\ ,\qquad\forall x\in\mathcal{L}U_{q}sl_{2}\ .

Now, express the l.h.s. of (
6.32
) in terms of K-operators and R-matrices. It reads:

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{\langle 12\rangle}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}{\bf K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{j+1})\mathcal{R}^{(\frac{1}{2},j)}(u^{2}q^{j+\frac{3}{2}}){\bf K}_{2}^{(j)}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\langle 12\rangle}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}}{\nu(uq^{j+1})\nu^{(j)}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mu^{(j)}(u^{2}q^{j+\frac{3}{2}}))}

\displaystyle=\frac{({\text{id}}\otimes\bar{\mathcal{F}}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})})\left[({\text{id}}\otimes\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}_{u^{-1}q^{-j-1}}\otimes\pi^{j}_{u^{-1}q^{-\frac{1}{2}}})({\text{id}}\otimes\Delta^{op})(\mathfrak{K})\right]({\text{id}}\otimes\bar{\mathcal{E}}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})})}{\nu(uq^{j+1})\nu^{(j)}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mu^{(j)}(u^{2}q^{j+\frac{3}{2}}))}

(6.35)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\overset{\eqref{int-op}}{=}\frac{\nu^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(u)}{\nu(uq^{j+1})\nu^{(j)}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mu^{(j)}(u^{2}q^{j+\frac{3}{2}}))}\mathcal{K}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(u)\ .

Then, simplifying the normalization factors and using (
6.3
), we get

\displaystyle\left[\nu(uq^{j})\nu(uq^{j-1})\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mu^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(u^{2}q^{j})\mu^{(j)}(u^{2}q^{j+\frac{3}{2}}))\right]^{-1}\mathcal{K}^{(j-\frac{1}{2})}(u)

\displaystyle=\left[\displaystyle{\prod_{k=0}^{2j-2}}\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mu(u^{2}q^{2j-k-1})\mu(u^{2}q^{2j-k}))\right]^{-1}\!\Gamma(uq^{j})\mathcal{K}^{(j-\frac{1}{2}})(u)\ .

Finally, using (
6.21
), the equation (
6.32
) follows. The relation (
6.33
) is obtained similarly.
∎

Secondly, we analyze the spin-
0
K-operator
{\bf K}^{(0)}(u)
and the analog of the quantum determinant (
5.19
) for the spin-
\frac{1}{2}
K-operator
{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
.

Proposition 6.11
.

Assume Conjecture
1
, then
{\bf K}^{(0)}(u)=1
. Furthermore, the normalized quantum determinant of the K-operator
{\bf K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)
is equally 1:

(6.36)

{\text{tr}}_{12}\big(\mathcal{P}_{12}^{-}{\bf K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(u)\mathcal{R}^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(qu^{2}){\bf K}_{2}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq)\big)=1\ .

Proof.

Specializing (
K3”
) to
j=\frac{1}{2}
we get

(6.37)

\displaystyle{\bf K}^{(0)}(u)

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{\langle 12\rangle}^{(0)}{\mathbf{K}}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{\frac{3}{2}})\mathcal{R}^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u^{2}q^{2}){\mathbf{K}}_{2}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\langle 12\rangle}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\nu(uq^{\frac{3}{2}})\nu(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mu(u^{2}q^{2}))\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{{\langle 12\rangle}}^{(0)}\mathcal{K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq^{\frac{3}{2}})R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u^{2}q^{2})\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{2}(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\langle 12\rangle}^{(0)}\ ,

where
\bar{\mathcal{E}}^{(0)}
,
\bar{\mathcal{F}}^{(0)}
are given by

(6.38)

\bar{\mathcal{E}}^{(0)}=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\\
-1\\
0\end{pmatrix},\qquad\bar{\mathcal{F}}^{(0)}=\begin{pmatrix}0&\frac{1}{2}&-\frac{1}{2}&0\\
\end{pmatrix}\ .

Then, noticing that for any two-by-two matrix
A
, one has the property:

(6.39)

\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{{\langle 12\rangle}}^{(0)}A\ \bar{\mathcal{E}}_{{\langle 12\rangle}}^{(0)}=\mathsf{tr}_{12}(\mathcal{P}_{12}^{-}A)\ ,

it follows from (
5.19
) that

(6.40)

\Gamma(u)=\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{{\langle 12\rangle}}^{(0)}\mathcal{K}_{1}^{(\frac{1}{2})}(uq)R^{(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}(u^{2}q)\mathcal{K}^{(\frac{1}{2})}_{2}(u)\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\langle 12\rangle}^{(0)}\ .

Therefore, the r.h.s. of (

) becomes

\displaystyle{\bf K}^{(0)}(u)

\displaystyle=\nu(uq^{\frac{3}{2}})\nu(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mu(u^{2}q^{2}))\Gamma(uq^{\frac{1}{2}})=1\ ,

where we used the functional relation (
6.3
).
We finally note that the quantum determinant in the l.h.s. of (

) is
{\bf K}^{(0)}(u)
, due to (
